chapter&#x27;s title, “The Sound of Nothing Happening,” chiefly refers to</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. the authorities immediately resolving every legal question.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. the public losing all interest in the rescue.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. the uneventful passing of a class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"90\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"92\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v08c09","title":"The Unfashionable Work of Being Free","volume":"卷八｜他们记得的世界","anchor":"chapter-0093","paragraphs":["Three weeks after the night at the conservatory, somebody painted Mara's face on the side of a building. The artist gave her a sword, a crown, and an expression of serene wisdom she had never possessed while awake.","Rowan arranged to stand beneath it when she came past.","\"A refreshing likeness,\" he said. \"You appear much better-behaved.\"","\"I shall have your portrait painted on the hospital's unpaid bills.\"","\"An ambitious cultural exchange.\"","She studied the crown. Children had already added a small rubber goose to one of its points. Rook claimed no responsibility, which June regarded as an insufficiently explicit denial.","The city was not uniformly grateful. One shopkeeper blamed the disruption for lost customers. An employee of an Ardent subsidiary feared that the investigation would cost her pension. A former official complained that the emergency had made ordinary work impossible, overlooking the months in which he had called other people's difficulties routine.","Mara listened more than the woman in the painting appeared inclined to do. Sometimes she could help. Sometimes she could explain what she did not control. Occasionally she had to leave a conversation unfinished and return to a task for which she had an actual skill.","The distinction was less glorious than saving a city in the newspapers. It was also how Mercy House's new reception desk acquired a drawer that opened properly.","She spent a morning repairing it with Ivo. He had been promoted, entirely against his will, into the unofficial manager of every broken object in the district. Anika had advised him to make a list, charge for work where appropriate, and stop accepting compliments as a form of payment.","\"A cruel influence,\" Mara told him.","\"She says you will agree with her.\"","\"An accurate one.\"","In the courtyard, patients and families were preparing a small festival. Nobody had intended to organize a production, but a choir had offered to sing, the old theater troupe wanted to perform a comedy, and the school astronomy club had requested space for a telescope. The boy carrying it introduced himself as an astronomer in training and asked whether he would have to wait until nightfall to see a useful star.","\"Usually,\" Mara said. \"But I have found that useful people dislike waiting for permission.\"","He looked concerned. \"I do need it to be dark.\"","She laughed and helped him find a safe place for the stand.","Inside the theater, the new Asterfall Philharmonic was rehearsing. The name had been chosen before the musicians had been counted. It now covered a surprisingly good violinist, two enthusiastic amateurs, a retired music teacher, and the child with the triangle. They were attempting a piece that required more strings than they owned.","Cassian had offered to pay for another instrument. Mara had told him to ask what the musicians actually wanted first. It turned out that they needed repairs, rehearsal space, and a kettle. He had supplied all three and was now carrying chairs without an audience.","She watched him from the doorway. The private pleasure of finding him there had not become smaller with familiarity.","\"You are looking at me,\" he said without turning.","\"I am assessing the maintenance program.\"","\"A favorable report, I hope.\"","She went to him and straightened the sleeve he had rolled badly. \"Promising. Some need for renewal.\"","He caught her hand and kissed the inside of her wrist. Behind them the violinist missed a note. Mara decided, charitably, that the instrument required further work.","The festival included an exhibition of objects recovered from the district's neglected buildings. A curator had borrowed space in the library to show an old theater program, a musician's annotated score, and a ledger recording gifts made by people whose names had disappeared from the official history of the institution.","Elena stood before the ledger for a long time. She recognized a repair on its cover. She did not recognize every entry.","Mara waited beside her.","\"I used to think forgetting a detail meant I had failed it,\" Elena said.","\"You have been very demanding company.\"","Her mother smiled. \"Historically.\"","They looked at the ledger again. A woman who had worked as a cleaner had paid for music lessons for three children. A sailor had given a month's income to replace a damaged roof. An anonymous donation had arrived with a note asking that no ceremony be held.","The city's annals had reserved more space for the men who cut ribbons than for the people who kept the buildings standing. June intended to write about that. The curator intended to amend the labels. Neither pretended an exhibition could complete the work.","Outside, the theater troupe began its comedy. It concerned a ruler who bought an expensive machine to tell him whether his subjects were happy. The machine reported increasing happiness whenever he threatened to replace it. The audience understood the joke before the ruler did.","Mara laughed until Cassian gave her his handkerchief.","\"A subtle satire,\" he said.","\"I admire its restraint.\"","At the edge of the crowd, a former Ardent employee watched without laughing. When the performance ended, she approached June and asked whether the paper would print a letter defending the people who had worked for the company without knowing what happened in its private rooms.","June listened. \"If the letter is yours, and the facts can be checked, yes.\"","Mara saw the woman's surprise. Freedom of expression sounded magnificent in a speech. Sometimes it became real in the less comfortable act of letting a person say something one's friends might dislike.","The woman sat down with a pen.","As the afternoon cooled, volunteers began taking the chairs inside. The astronomer in training returned for his telescope. Cassian helped him carry it to the courtyard while Mara took her mother back to the clinic.","At the door, Elena touched the silver flower pinned inside Mara's coat.","\"It was never meant to be a weapon.\"","\"I know.\"","\"It was meant to help someone find where the work had gone wrong.\"","Mara closed her hand around it. Missing Ring","volume":"卷八｜他们记得的世界","anchor":"chapter-0094","paragraphs":["Cassian was behaving suspiciously.","Mara had tried to be reasonable about it. A man could ask Rowan to leave a parcel with Mrs. Wren without planning a deception. He could also ask Ivo whether a certain mechanism could be repaired by Friday. The two requests might be unrelated. Her suspicion became less generous when June stopped talking the moment she entered the room.","\"We were discussing the weather,\" June said.","Mara looked out at a sunny courtyard. \"A controversial subject.\"","\"The forecast is speculative.\"","\"So is your talent for lying.\"","June took her notebook and escaped.","For two days Mara watched the conspiracy develop. The theater received a delivery of flowers. Mrs. Wren inspected a length of ribbon and refused to explain its purpose. Ivo wore the expression of a stressed-out professional who had promised a repair before examining what the client had brought him.","Mara considered asking Anika. Then she saw Anika kiss Ivo behind the workshop door and decided that one private scandal at a time was sufficient.","By Friday, she had reached three possible explanations. Cassian was planning a public reception, an elaborate birthday celebration several months early, or a proposal. The last possibility made her so inconveniently happy that she distrusted her own analysis.","She attempted to inquire indirectly.","\"Do you believe people should be warned before an important event?\" she asked over breakfast.","Cassian looked at her above his cup. \"In principle.\"","\"A reassuring pronouncement.\"","\"Are you planning one?\"","\"I am attempting to determine whether you are.\"","He put down the cup. \"Would you prefer to know?\"","There it was, the open door again. She could ask, and he would tell her. She could also allow him the pleasure of doing something foolishly romantic without examining every corner first.","\"I would prefer,\" she said carefully, \"not to be surprised in front of a large crowd.\"","\"A modest request.\"","\"And no speeches about acquiring me.\"","His mouth curved. \"I have insufficient storage.\"","She threw a piece of bread at him. He caught it, which annoyed her more than it should have.","That evening he met her outside the theater. He wore an ordinary dark coat. She wore the dress she liked rather than the one everybody else called impressive. He offered his arm, and they walked through the empty lobby into the little courtyard behind the stage.","There were flowers. Not a wall of them, not an arrangement designed to dazzle an audience. A few climbing plants had been moved to frame the old bench. Two cups stood on a table beside a covered dish. Somewhere in the building, a musician was playing softly enough that the sound might have been a rehearsal.","\"No crowd,\" he said.","\"You have shown restraint.\"","\"I am hoping for favorable feedback.\"","She sat. He uncovered the dish. Inside lay the two little cakes from the hospital café.","Mara looked at him and immediately lost the ability to produce a clever answer.","\"I thought we could begin with something reliable,\" he said.","They ate the cakes. She told him that the university had invited her to teach a short conservation course. He told her he had declined an offer to return immediately to his former position. He wanted the inquiry into his own case finished before accepting another office whose authority might complicate it.","Neither decision solved the rest of their lives. They discussed travel, work, Elena's recovery, and the possibility of a home in which a person could leave a half-repaired clock on the table without being asked whether it was dangerous.","\"That last condition may require negotiation,\" Cassian said.","\"It is important to my lifelong happiness.\"","\"Then I shall have to learn to identify the dangerous ones.\"","She took his hand beneath the table. \"I could teach you.\"","\"I had hoped you might.\"","At last he stood and moved around the table. His expression had become familiar in a new way: the look he wore when he could not make a difficult thing easier by preparing for it.","\"Mara Vale,\" he said, \"I love you. I would like to spend my life with you, including the parts neither of us can plan. Will you marry me?\"","She had known the question might come. The knowing did nothing to prepare her for hearing it.","\"Yes,\" she said.","He closed his eyes for a moment. When he opened them, the relief on his face was so unguarded that she stood and put her arms around him.","\"You appear to have survived the answer,\" she whispered.","\"I may require a moment.\"","She kissed him. Somewhere behind the theater door, something made a small, muffled crash.","They both turned.","\"No crowd?\" Mara asked.","Cassian looked towards the door with a resignation that made her begin to laugh.","\"A failure of enforcement.\"","Ivo's voice came through the wood. \"It was the tray. We were leaving.\"","\"You were not,\" Anika said.","Mrs. Wren opened the door. Rowan stood behind her holding an empty glass. June was not even pretending to be ashamed. Noor carried a small box.","\"He forgot this,\" she said, handing it to Cassian.","For the first time since Mara had known him, Cassian looked genuinely disorganized.","The box contained a plain ring set with a small, pale stone. It was beautiful without resembling Selene's diamond. On the inside, a short engraving read: COME HOME AS YOURSELF.","Mara touched the letters with her fingertip.","\"You had it made?\"","\"Repaired,\" he said. \"The band belonged to my grandmother. I thought you might prefer something with a history we could acknowledge rather than improve.\"","She held out her hand. He put the ring on it, then kissed the place where it rested.","Rowan sighed. \"I have lost a valuable source of entertainment. He will be unbearably happy.\"","\"You may survive it,\" Mara told him.","June began to applaud. The others joined her, absurdly and warmly, in the little courtyard where nobody had acquired anybody and everyone appeared delighted by the arrangement.","Later, after the cups were empty and their friends had finally gone, Mara sat beside Cassian on the bench. She turned the ring once, watching the stone catch the last light.","\"You realize I could have stolen it,\" she said.","\"I was relying on you to prefer the question.\"","She leaned against him. \"An excellent gamble.\""],"questions":[{"type":"推断","stem":"The grandmother's repaired ring suits Mara chiefly because it","options":["restores the memories removed during the earlier trials.","carries an stole her sign. She stood in the narrow street, holding a ribbon and a pair of ceremonial scissors, and considered the awkward possibility that the city had developed a sense of humor specifically to torment her.","\"A professional criticism,\" Cassian suggested.","\"Of my painting?\"","\"Of your security.\"","The missing sign had announced VALE RESTORATION in plain blue letters. It had cost her three evenings, a bruised thumb, and an argument with Rowan, who had proposed lettering visible from neighboring countries. Beneath the empty bracket, however, somebody had left a smaller board: LOOK IN THE WINDOW.","Inside stood the original sign, repaired where the wood had split, with a narrow silver star fixed above the V. Elena waited beside it, visibly nervous. She had asked the carpenter to do the heavy work. Her own contribution had been the star, shaped slowly over several afternoons, and she was watching Mara with the fearful concentration of someone offering a gift whose meaning might be larger than its size.","\"Your aunt said a surprise should involve an obstacle,\" Elena explained.","\"My aunt considers a wedding an obstacle race.\"","\"Shall I put it back?\"","Mara set down the scissors and embraced her. The star pressed cool against her wrist. Neither woman apologized for crying. Through the glass, June photographed the ribbon, then lowered her camera before the embrace became a public possession.","The studio occupied the ground floor of a former bakery. One door faced the busy street; the other opened into a courtyard shared with a bicycle repairer and an elderly music teacher. Mara had chosen the two entrances deliberately. Wealthy clients could bring valuable furniture through the front, but the school caretaker with a broken cabinet would receive the same welcome at the back. Prices would differ with the job. Courtesy would not.","Her first customer was Mrs. Dallow, who arrived carrying a chair that had belonged to her husband. She did not want it made fashionable. She wanted the loose arm fixed, the dark mark left by his pipe preserved, and the seat strengthened sufficiently for a grandchild to climb onto it.","Mara wrote the instructions down. Restoration, she told her apprentice, was not an excuse to impose the restorer's taste. The chair was allowed to show its age. Indeed, removing every sign of use would destroy the very history Mrs. Dallow had brought it to them to save.","Her apprentice, Ada, nodded. She had left the academy's temporary research post for a three-day working arrangement that still allowed her to study. On her first morning she labeled two jars incorrectly, discovered the error herself, and expected a reprimand. Mara gave her fresh labels.","\"We correct it before it harms the work,\" she said. \"Then we find out why it happened. Shame is a poor storage system.\"","At noon, the bicycle repairer arrived with a gift: a bell that rang whenever either door opened. He had tried to make the two sounds different, but they were so similar that only his grandson could distinguish them. For the rest of the week, every entrance caused Ada to announce, \"Either a client or a bicycle.\"","Cassian came by after his interview with the independent inquiry. He had given evidence about decisions that had helped the rescue and about decisions he wished he had questioned sooner. The inquiry would take months. Giving an honest account did not entitle him to choose the findings, and Mara loved him more for understanding that.","There were consequences for others as well. The city suspended the company's trial permissions while evidence was examined. Some workers found new jobs; others remained unemployed and angry. A compensation fund began accepting claims, with an independent process for disputing decisions. It could pay certain expenses. It could not reimburse a person for every lost conversation or settle every argument about what a damaged record meant.","Selene's lawyers challenged parts of the investigation. June reported their arguments along with the evidence against her. The story had become less spectacular to read and more important to follow. A prison sentence, if one came after a fair trial, would not itself repair a hospital budget or tell Tomas how to speak to the daughter he had missed.","That afternoon, Tomas arrived with Leda. They brought a wooden music box whose handle had snapped. Leda did the talking at first. Tomas listened, then corrected one small detail about where the box had stood in their old home. She stared at him for a moment, surprised and pleased, before asking whether he remembered the tune.","He did not. Mara did not tell them that he surely would. She promised to mend the handle and let the box supply its own music.","When they left, Cassian stood beside the workbench, turning a scrap of wood between his fingers. \"I had prepared a present,\" he said.","\"Is it a continent? Rowan already offered me one. I said the heating would be expensive.\"","He placed a small brass plate on the bench. It bore no crest and no title. YOUR WORK. YOUR NAME. Beneath the words were two holes for screws.","Mara ran a thumb along the smooth edge. \"I like the punctuation.\"","\"I argued with the engraver for twenty minutes.\"","\"A useful preparation for marriage.\"","They fixed the plate inside the courtyard door, where she would see it each morning before meeting a client. Then he helped her lift Mrs. Dallow's chair onto the bench. The world had supplied enough declarations. A loose arm still needed mending.","At dusk, Noor arrived with supper, Ivo with a clock that displayed the wrong hour attractively, and Rowan with a bottle whose price Mara refused to learn. Anika came in her working boots. She and Ivo had been exchanging letters about engines, which everybody understood to be a deeply inefficient method of discussing their feelings.","\"I have leave next month,\" Anika told him.","Ivo glanced at his clock, as if next month might be found somewhere among its confused hands. \"I have a roof garden. No sea.\"","\"I can manage a garden.\"","\"There are stairs.\"","\"Ivo. I command a tanker.\"","He smiled so broadly that June looked away to give him the courtesy of not being watched.","They ate around the workbench after covering the chair and washing their hands. Elena grew tired and went home with Noor before the others. Mara felt the old impulse to follow, to check every door and every possible danger. Instead she asked when her mother would like to come again.","\"Tuesday,\" Elena said. \"Unless I prefer Wednesday.\"","\"Send word. Either will do.\"","The bell rang behind them. Two entrances, almost the same sound. Mara thought that a life could grow larger without becoming noisier. Sometimes it merely needed another door, and the courage to leave room for someone else's choice."],"questions":[{"type":"论证结构","stem":"Mrs. Dallow's instructions about her chair show why successful restoration may require","options":["removing every visible sign that an object has been used.","preserving signs of personal history while repairing a functional weakness.","replacing a client's Controlled","volume":"卷八｜他们记得的世界","anchor":"chapter-0096","paragraphs":["The wedding was scheduled for three. At a quarter past two, Cassian discovered a goat in the room reserved for the rings.","It was a small white goat with a blue ribbon, a calm expression, and one corner of the seating plan in its mouth. Captain Rook had brought it as a replacement for the rubber goose that Ottilie had forbidden at the ceremony. Her reasoning, when demanded, was that nobody had forbidden a goat.","\"You are a retired captain,\" Cassian said.","\"Retirement leaves room for development.\"","From beyond the door came Rowan's voice. \"If anyone asks, this is an ancient family custom.\"","\"If anyone asks,\" Cassian replied, \"you are responsible for the ancient family custom.\"","In another part of the house, Mara was trying to keep still while Ada fastened the last buttons of her dress. It was ivory, beautifully cut, and equipped with pockets. The dressmaker had resisted the pockets until Mara demonstrated how much a bride might need to carry: a handkerchief, a key, a pencil, and an emergency biscuit.","\"You have forgotten the lock pick,\" Ada said.","\"That would be improper at my wedding.\"","\"You have two.\"","Mara smiled at her reflection. Once she had looked in mirrors primarily to check a disguise. Today she recognized the woman looking back and felt no impulse to improve the identification.","Elena sat near the window. She wore a blue silk scarf and held a small photograph whose edges had softened with age. It showed Mara at eight, furious because somebody had insisted she put down a broken toy before the picture was taken. Elena could recall the toy, though not the name of the person who had taken the photograph. She said so without apologizing.","Mara knelt beside her. \"I don't need today to recover everything.\"","\"Good,\" Elena said. \"I intend to remember only the flattering parts of your speech.\"","They laughed, and the laughter loosened something that no reassurance could have untied. Elena reached up to straighten a strand of her daughter's hair. This ordinary gesture, familiar and uncertain at once, was enough.","The ceremony took place in the courtyard behind the restored theater. The building had survived scandal, neglect, a rescue operation, and Gideon Pike's opinions about modern lighting. It could survive a wedding. Children had hung paper stars between the upper windows. The gardener had left a patch of wildflowers by the wall because nobody could agree that it would look better as bare stone.","There were no newspaper seats. June had agreed to take a few photographs chosen by the couple, then attend as a friend. Public curiosity did not create a public right to the words Mara and Cassian meant for each other.","The goat was removed from the ring room. The seating plan was repaired with transparent tape. A cousin who had demanded a more important position discovered that Mrs. Wren had placed him beside a thoughtful six-year-old, who asked why his medals looked like wrapped sweets. For once, he had no prepared response.","When Mara came into the courtyard, Cassian forgot the first sentence of his carefully prepared greeting. He remembered her first night at the auction: the impossible composure, the stolen flower, the certainty that she knew something he needed to learn. Since then he had seen her frightened, furious, exhausted, kind, mistaken, and brave. None of it made this moment smaller.","\"Hello,\" he said when she reached him.","\"An excellent beginning.\"","\"I had something longer.\"","\"Save it. We have time.\"","The official read the necessary words. They listened. Mara had no interest in making a promise impressive by making it impossible. She could not promise never to be angry, never to leave a room, or always to understand him without explanation. She could promise to return to a difficult conversation, to respect his decisions, and to ask for the same respect in return.","Cassian promised to stand beside her without confusing love with possession. He would not treat her independence as a trial he might eventually finish. He would tell her when he was afraid. He would let her know him when he felt least worthy of being known.","\"And,\" he added, abandoning the remaining prepared lines, \"I will try to stop putting the keys in the sugar bowl.\"","Mara blinked. \"That was you?\"","Several witnesses lost their composure. The official waited with professional patience while the bride recovered enough to make the corresponding promise about leaving tools on the stairs.","The rings were where they ought to be, although Rowan looked personally proud of this modest achievement. Cassian slid the repaired family ring onto Mara's finger. Its words rested against her skin: COME HOME AS YOURSELF.","She gave him a plain band with a tiny star engraved inside. He bent his head to hear what she said as she placed it on his hand.","\"I chose you when there were no witnesses. I'm pleased to choose you in front of them.\"","The kiss that followed was sufficiently convincing to silence even the cousin with the medals. Then Captain Rook applauded too early, the goat answered from the passage, and the courtyard surrendered to laughter.","At the reception, the first dance went almost smoothly. Mara caught a heel in a fold of her dress; Cassian caught Mara; together they turned the accident into a movement nobody dared describe as deliberate. Rowan declared it the latest fashion. Within ten minutes three other couples were attempting it, with results that justified the presence of a doctor.","Noor refused to organize a clinic beside the cake. \"Walk,\" she instructed the dancers. \"You may discover it has advantages.\"","Tomas and Leda sat with Amina beneath the paper stars. Elena joined them when the music grew too loud. On the other side of the courtyard, Ivo and Anika were discussing their garden visit. The conversation now contained very little about engines. June wrote no notes. Mrs. Wren accepted a second slice of cake after firmly refusing the first, which everybody understood as a familiar stage in the process.","Later, Mara found herself alone for a minute in the theater passage. The old painted scenery smelled faintly of dust and fresh timber. She touched the silver flower pinned near her shoulder. It had once been a clue she hoped would lead to a single answer. Now it was an object with several histories: her mother's work, a theft, a warning, a courtroom receipt, a repair. None canceled the others.","Cassian found her there. \"Running away?\"","\"Considering whether the cake is worth returning for.\"","\"There are two kinds.\"","\"A strong argument.\"","He held out his hand. She took it, and for a moment neither moved. Beyond the open door, their friends were arguing over the music. Beyond the courtyard lay a city that still needed money, honest work, patient care, and the occasional person willing to ask why a locked door existed. Loving it did not require believing it perfect.","A messenger arrived the next morning, when the wedding flowers were still in their vases and the household had not quite recovered. A small provincial museum had lost a painting. A replacement hung in its place, so skillfully aged that the curator was no longer sure which of his records could be trusted. He asked whether Mara Vale might advise him.","Cassian read the letter over her shoulder. \"We had planned a quiet journey.\"","\"A museum can be quiet.\"","\"Mara.\"","\"An extremely quiet theft, perhaps.\"","He laughed, kissed her hair, and went to find the map. She finished her tea before following him. Once, she had needed to steal a place in the world. Now she had work worth doing, people who could choose to stay, and a man who knew that an invitation to adventure might arrive disguised as an entirely respectable letter.","She locked the studio, checked that Ada had the spare key, and stepped into the morning. Her husband was waiting at the corner with two tickets, one suitcase, and the expression of a man who had willingly married a complication.","\"Ready?\" he asked.","Mara took his arm. \"Let's find out what happens next.\""],"questions":[{"type":"推断","stem":"What do Mara's wedding promises suggest about her understanding of commitment?","options":["Independence is a temporary difficulty that
